stems = {}


stems["P1_U3_SET_01"] = (
    "A boutique beverage manufacturer operates as a Private Limited Company, producing organic cold-pressed juices sold in 250ml glass bottles. "
    "Operating for 4 years, the business supplies 45 local health food stores and generates $600,000 in annual revenue at a unit price of $4.00 per bottle, "
    "holding a 5% share of the regional premium juice market. Total annual marketing expenses are budgeted at $35,000.\n\n"
    "To expand overall sales, the company is developing a new line of functional wellness shots. The marketing director must decide whether to target "
    "a narrow niche market of health-conscious gym members or attempt mass market distribution through national supermarket chains. Additionally, "
    "the business is deciding between allocating $20,000 towards social media video advertisements targeted at urban consumers aged 18–35 or conducting "
    "in-store taste testing trials across major retail grocery locations."
)


stems["P1_U3_SET_02"] = (
    "An eco-friendly footwear company operates as a Public Limited Company, manufacturing sustainable sneakers made from recycled ocean plastics. "
    "Operating for 5 years, the firm generates $3.2m in annual sales revenue, selling 40,000 pairs annually at an average retail price of $80 per pair. "
    "The business currently holds an 8% market share in the sustainable athletic footwear segment, operating with an annual marketing budget of $180,000.\n\n"
    "To drive market share growth against traditional footwear brands, executive directors are evaluating market segmentation strategies. "
    "The marketing team is debating whether to segment the consumer market by demographic factors like income level and age groups or by psychographic "
    "lifestyle factors targeting environmentally conscious urban commuters. Furthermore, the company must choose between launching a mass market "
    "billboard campaign or focusing promotional spend on targeted influencer marketing on photo-sharing platforms."
)


stems["P1_U3_SET_03"] = (
    "A electric bicycle manufacturer operates as a Private Limited Company, producing commuter e-bikes priced at $1,200 per unit. Operating for 6 years, "
    "the firm sells 2,500 e-bikes annually through 15 independent bicycle dealerships, generating $3.0m in annual revenue with an 11% segment market share. "
    "The company allocates an annual marketing research budget of $50,000.\n\n"
    "To design a lighter folding e-bike for urban office workers, the product development team requires detailed data on consumer preferences regarding "
    "battery range and frame weight. The marketing manager is debating market research methods: conducting primary market research via online consumer surveys "
    "and focus groups with commuter cyclists, or relying on secondary market research from published government transport reports and industry trade publications. "
    "The manager must balance data accuracy and specific relevance against research cost and implementation speed."
)


stems["P1_U3_SET_04"] = (
    "A gourmet ice cream producer operates as a Partnership, manufacturing organic dairy ice cream tubs sold in 500ml containers at $6.50 per unit. "
    "Operating for 3 years, the enterprise supplies 60 specialty grocery shops and generates $450,000 in annual sales volume, holding a 4% share of the "
    "regional specialty dessert market. The firm maintains an annual marketing research budget of $15,000.\n\n"
    "Before introducing new plant-based vegan flavors, the partners need feedback on taste preferences and price tolerance. The marketing lead is deciding "
    "on consumer sampling techniques: using random sampling across general supermarket shoppers or stratified sampling targeting existing buyers of organic "
    "and dairy-free food products. The partners must determine which sampling method delivers the most reliable data while keeping market research expenses "
    "within their strict $15,000 budget constraint."
)


stems["P1_U3_SET_05"] = (
    "A consumer electronics firm operates as a Public Limited Company, manufacturing wireless noise-canceling headphones priced at $150 per unit. "
    "Operating for 7 years, the business generates $8.5m in annual sales revenue, holding a 12% market share in personal audio devices. "
    "The firm allocates an annual promotional budget of $400,000 across multiple electronic product lines.\n\n"
    "Sales figures reveal that its flagship headphone model has entered the saturation stage of the product life cycle, with monthly sales volume dropping "
    "by 15% over the last two quarters due to aggressive competitor launches. According to the Boston Matrix, the product risks turning into a cash dog. "
    "The brand manager must evaluate extension strategies to prolong product life, choosing between redesigning product packaging with eco-friendly "
    "materials or launching an updated model featured with improved battery life and new color finishes."
)

    "or apply cost-plus pricing to ensure a stable 25% profit margin per ride while keeping passenger fares predictable."

    "To expand sales volume into new geographic regions, the directors are evaluating channels of distribution. The sales manager is comparing two distribution routes: "
    "selling directly to end consumers via an e-commerce website (direct distribution channel) or distributing through regional grocery wholesalers who resell "

)

    "The company operates with an annual promotional budget of $250,000.\n\n"
    "The marketing department is preparing a major promotional campaign for the upcoming holiday sales season. The marketing director is comparing two promotional "

```python
    "A cosmetics manufacturing firm operates as a Private Limited Company, producing organic skincare creams packaged in 50ml glass jars priced at $35 per unit. "
    "Operating for 8 years, the enterprise generates $2.4m in annual revenue, distributing products through 80 retail beauty boutiques and holding a 6% market share "
    "in natural cosmetics. The company maintains an annual branding and packaging budget of $120,000.\n\n"
    "To stand out on crowded retail store shelves and increase customer brand loyalty, management is reviewing its overall product strategy. The marketing director "
    "is analyzing whether investing in premium, eco-friendly biodegradable glass packaging will strengthen brand image and justify price increases, or whether establishing "
```

```python
# SET 14 (3.2 Sampling Methods & Accuracy)
stem_14 = (

    "To evaluate launching a new line of sugar-free energy infusions, the management team must gather consumer preference data. "
```

```python
stem_19_expanded = (
    "A handcrafted leather footwear business operates as a Private Limited Company, manufacturing bespoke leather boots priced at $180 per pair. "
    "Operating for 7 years, the firm generates $2.7m in annual revenue, selling 15,000 pairs annually through 25 independent fashion boutiques. "
    "The business holds a 5% market share in luxury footwear, maintaining an annual distribution and marketing budget of $140,000.\n\n"
    "To expand sales volume globally and improve gross profit margins, executive directors are evaluating international distribution channels. "
    "The marketing manager proposes transitioning from selling through boutique retailers (two-stage distribution channel) to launching a direct-to-consumer e-commerce website. "
    "The manager must weigh higher profit margins and direct customer relationship control against increased international shipping logistics, digital advertising, and online product return costs."
)


set_19["scenario_stem"] = stem_19_expanded


for i, s in enumerate(sets, start=11):
    wc = count_words(s["scenario_stem"])
    print(f"Set {i} stem word count: {wc}")
    assert 120 <= wc <= 180, f"Set {i} word count {wc} out of range!"


json_output = json.dumps(sets, indent=2)
print("Valid JSON string length:", len(json_output))
```

Section 4


def word_count(text):
    return len(text.split())


stems = {}


# Set 01: 4.1 Production Methods & Technology (Job to Batch/Flow, JIT)
stems["P1_U4_SET_01"] = (
    "A manufacturing company operates as a Private Limited Company, producing custom packaging boxes in a single factory employing 80 production workers. "
    "Operating for 5 years, the factory uses batch production to manufacture 100,000 cardboard boxes per week. The factory incurs weekly fixed costs of $15,000 "
    "and total variable costs of $45,000, selling each box at a wholesale price of $0.80. Due to manual assembly errors, the current defect rate is 4%, leading "
    "to wasted raw materials and customer complaints.\n\n"
    "To improve efficiency, the operations manager is considering investing $350,000 in an automated flow production line. Alternatively, the company is "
    "evaluating adopting Lean Production techniques, specifically Just-In-Time (JIT) inventory management, to eliminate raw material storage costs and reduce "
    "cardboard waste across the assembly plant."
)


# Set 02: 4.1 Production Methods & Technology (Job vs Batch / CAD/CAM)
stems["P1_U4_SET_02"] = (
    "A specialized furniture workshop operates as a Partnership, handcrafting wooden dining tables in a small workshop with 12 skilled carpenters. "
    "Operating for 8 years, the workshop utilizes job production to complete 50 bespoke tables per week. Weekly fixed costs are $8,000, while total variable "
    "costs reach $22,000. Each custom table sells at a retail price of $800, but manual woodworking causes a 5% defect rate that requires costly rework.\n\n"
    "To satisfy rising commercial restaurant orders, the partners are evaluating operational changes. The production head proposes transitioning from job "
    "production to batch production using Computer-Aided Manufacturing (CAM) equipment costing $120,000. Alternatively, the firm is considering expanding "
    "job production capacity by hiring 8 additional skilled carpenters and introducing a Total Quality Management (TQM) framework."
)


# Set 03: 4.2 Costs & Average Cost Calculations (Fixed, Variable, Total, Average Cost)
stems["P1_U4_SET_03"] = (
    "A commercial bakery operates as a Private Limited Company, producing frozen pizza crusts for grocery chains. Operating for 6 years, the plant employs "
    "45 factory workers and operates two automated baking lines. The facility produces 60,000 pizza crusts weekly, incurring weekly fixed costs of $24,000 "
    "and total variable costs of $36,000 for flour, yeast, and packaging. The wholesale price per pizza crust is $1.20, with an operational defect rate of 3%.\n\n"
    "To lower average unit costs and expand weekly production capacity to 90,000 units, the operations director is reviewing cost structures. "
    "The director must decide whether to invest $200,000 in a high-speed flour mixing machine to cut variable labor costs or relocate production to a cheaper "
    "industrial zone with lower weekly lease costs."
)


# Set 04: 4.2 Costs & Average Cost Calculations (Cost analysis & productivity)
stems["P1_U4_SET_04"] = (
    "A textile manufacturing firm operates as a Public Limited Company, producing denim jeans in a dedicated garment factory. Operating for 10 years, "
    "the plant employs 150 sewing machine operators and produces 20,000 pairs of jeans weekly. Weekly factory fixed costs stand at $50,000, while total variable "
    "costs equal $110,000. The company sells jeans to retail buyers at $12.00 per unit, facing a 4% defect rate due to stitching errors.\n\n"
    "With rising fabric prices pushing total variable costs higher, executive directors are evaluating operational strategies to reduce average unit costs. "
    "The plant manager proposes introducing performance-related pay for sewing operators to boost labor productivity, while the operations chief advocates "
    "installing automated laser cutting machines costing $400,000 to eliminate material fabric waste."
)


# Set 05: 4.2 Break-Even Analysis & Economies of Scale (Break-Even, Margin of Safety)
stems["P1_U4_SET_05"] = (
    "A beverage bottling plant operates as a Private Limited Company, producing canned sparkling water. Operating for 4 years, the factory employs 35 workers "
    "and produces 120,000 aluminum cans per week. The plant incurs weekly fixed costs of $30,000, total variable costs of $48,000, and sells each canned drink "
    "at $0.90 per unit. Operating with a 2% defect rate, the factory currently operates close to its break-even point.\n\n"
    "To increase the margin of safety, the operations team is analyzing break-even metrics. Management is debating whether to reduce variable costs by switching "
    "to cheaper aluminum casing suppliers or increase weekly output to 180,000 cans by adding a night shift, taking advantage of purchasing economies of scale."
)


# Set 06: 4.2 Break-Even Analysis & Economies of Scale (Economies vs Diseconomies)
stems["P1_U4_SET_06"] = (
    "An electric bicycle assembly plant operates as a Public Limited Company, manufacturing commuter e-bikes. Operating for 7 years, the facility employs "
    "110 assembly technicians and produces 1,000 e-bikes per week. Weekly fixed costs total $80,000, and total variable costs reach $320,000 for motors, "
    "batteries, and frames. Each e-bike sells at a wholesale price of $500, with a 3% defect rate during final quality checks.\n\n"
    "To achieve financial economies of scale, the board wants to double weekly factory output to 2,000 units. However, the plant manager warns that rapid expansion "
    "in a single facility could trigger managerial diseconomies of scale due to communication breakdowns. The board must choose between expanding the existing factory "
    "or building a second smaller assembly facility."
)


# Set 07: 4.3 Achieving Quality Production (Quality Control vs Quality Assurance)
stems["P1_U4_SET_07"] = (
    "A medical device manufacturer operates as a Private Limited Company, producing disposable plastic syringes in a sterile cleanroom facility. "
    "Operating for 9 years, the plant employs 60 cleanroom technicians and produces 250,000 syringes weekly. Weekly fixed costs are $40,000, and total variable "
    "costs equal $75,000. Syringes are sold to hospital distributors at $0.60 each. Inspection reports reveal a 5% defect rate during end-of-line quality control checks.\n\n"
    "Because defective medical syringes endanger patient safety and cause costly product recalls, the managing director demands operational quality improvements. "
    "The quality manager recommends replacing end-of-line Quality Control with a comprehensive Quality Assurance system across all assembly stages. Alternatively, "
    "the firm is considering investing $180,000 in automated optical sensor inspection equipment."
)


# Set 08: 4.3 Achieving Quality Production (Total Quality Management / Kaizen)
stems["P1_U4_SET_08"] = (
    "An automotive component manufacturer operates as a Public Limited Company, producing steel brake discs for car assembly plants. Operating for 12 years, "
    "the foundry employs 200 factory workers and produces 40,000 brake discs per week. The plant incurs weekly fixed costs of $120,000 and total variable "
    "costs of $280,000. Each brake disc sells for $14.00, but metal casting flaws result in a 4% defect rate.\n\n"
    "Automotive clients have warned that future contracts require zero defects. The operations head must choose a quality management framework: implementing "
    "Total Quality Management (TQM) with continuous improvement (Kaizen) groups across the factory floor, or relying on strict Quality Control inspection teams "
    "stationed at every manufacturing workstation."
)


# Set 09: 4.4 Location Decisions (Manufacturing Location Factors)
stems["P1_U4_SET_09"] = (
    "A chemical processing enterprise operates as a Private Limited Company, manufacturing liquid industrial cleaning detergents. Operating for 6 years, "
    "the processing plant employs 50 industrial workers and produces 80,000 liters of detergent per week. Weekly fixed costs are $35,000, total variable "
    "costs are $85,000, and the wholesale price is $2.00 per liter. The plant experiences a 2% spillage defect rate during bottle filling.\n\n"
    "With urban residential development surrounding its current site, lease costs have surged. The operations director is deciding on factory relocation. "
    "The options are moving to an industrial estate near major highway networks and chemical raw material suppliers, or relocating offshore to a developing country "
    "with 40% lower labor costs but higher international freight transport expenses."
)


# Set 10: 4.4 Location Decisions (Service/Retail Location Factors)
stems["P1_U4_SET_10"] = (
    "A regional logistics and fulfillment business operates as a Private Limited Company, managing e-commerce parcel distribution. Operating for 5 years, "
    "the central warehouse employs 90 warehouse operators and processes 150,000 parcels per week. Weekly fixed costs stand at $60,000, with total variable costs "
    "reaching $135,000. The company charges e-commerce retailers an average fee of $1.80 per parcel processed, facing a 3% sorting error defect rate.\n\n"
    "To handle growing parcel volumes and meet next-day delivery promises, the board is evaluating warehouse location strategy. The options are relocating "
    "to a large suburban logistics hub adjacent to a major airport and cargo rail terminal, or leasing three smaller regional fulfillment centers closer to urban "
    "shopping centers."
)


for k, v in stems.items():
    print(f"{k}: {word_count(v)} words")

```python
stems["P1_U4_SET_05"] = (

    "To increase the margin of safety and protect profit margins, the operations team is analyzing break-even metrics. Management is debating whether to reduce "

)
```

```python
# Set 13: 4.2 Costs & Average Cost Calculations (Fixed, Variable, Total, Average Cost)
stem_13 = (
    "A commercial cardboard container factory operates as a Private Limited Company, manufacturing heavy-duty corrugated shipping boxes for industrial clients. "
```

```python
# Set 15: 4.2 Break-Even Analysis & Economies of Scale (Break-Even, Margin of Safety)
stem_15 = (
    "A commercial paint manufacturing facility operates as a Private Limited Company, producing interior wall paint packaged in 5-liter plastic buckets. "

    "cause a 2% spillage defect rate. The factory currently operates close to its break-even point.\n\n"
    "To increase the margin of safety and protect against raw material price volatility, management is analyzing break-even metrics. "
    "The operations team is debating whether to reduce variable costs by sourcing lower-grade chemical resins, or increase weekly production output to 75,000 buckets "
    "by running weekend shifts to secure bulk purchasing economies of scale on chemical orders."
)
```

```python
# Set 18: 4.3 Achieving Quality Production (Total Quality Management / Kaizen)
```
